# The known population of 1 July 1835

Generated by `tools/profile_population_1835.py --build`. Every figure is re-derived; `--check` proves this file is what the layer says. Ticket T-1160, as of 2026-09-18.

> A PROFILE OF THE EVIDENCE, NOT OF THE TOWN. 2265 person entries in 1393 households against a town census of 3,265 people. What is thin below is the record, and the tickets named against each hole are what fill it.

*THE TABLE ABOVE IS THE STRICTEST READING AND IT IS NOT THE POPULATION (T-1386). 820 of these households stand `uncertain` on the card because no record follows the person to 1 July 1835 — unadjudicated, never disputed. Every one of them has attested or inferred evidence of living in this town and none has evidence of being elsewhere that day, so all 833 of their people are ruled into the town, each at the tier its own readings reach. Exactly 2 cards are out on evidence of absence. The ruling is data/reconstruction/1835_presence_rulings.json; the cards keep the inferred `uncertain` the research wrote.*

*No PERSON carries the `reconstructed` grade (980) and this ticket does not move that: reconstruction begins at T-1167 under its own programme. But 302 ATTRIBUTE values already do, and a person-grade count hides them — which is the whole reason T-1158 puts a tier on the field rather than on the card.*

## Per-attribute completeness

THE NINE QUESTIONS ASKED OF EVERY ONE OF THE 2265 PEOPLE, TOGETHER. 6 of the nine axes leave nobody unanswered. Sex leaves 6 and age band 7 — the collective rows, "the rest of the Beaubien household, unnamed" and their kind, which name nobody and so can carry neither, and one row that names one woman and still earns no band; every one of them is named below. Division leaves 1408, the people of the 1186 households no source puts on a side of the river, and that is the layer's largest remaining hole: T-1198 seats the households the evidence places and T-1199 the reconstructed ones, and until they run this column cannot read zero without inventing ground. 850 people are answered on all nine. Two axes answer in a bare string that carries no confidence anywhere on the card — they are counted under "stated, no tier written" and not under a tier, because a value with no tier is not a value at a tier.

### Every person, every attribute

| attribute | carried by | answered | share | attested | inferred | reconstructed | stated, no tier written | not answered |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| sex | person | 2259 | 99.7% | 1082 | 590 | 587 | 0 | 6 |
| age band | person | 2258 | 99.7% | 7 | 54 | 2197 | 0 | 7 |
| arrival | household | 2265 | 100.0% | 127 | 1445 | 693 | 0 | 0 |
| origin | household | 2265 | 100.0% | 24 | 84 | 2157 | 0 | 0 |
| reason for coming | household | 2265 | 100.0% | 33 | 28 | 2204 | 0 | 0 |
| a role, or a stated reason for having none | person | 2265 | 100.0% | 253 | 74 | 1938 | 0 | 0 |
| presence on the scene date | household | 2265 | 100.0% | 59 | 1520 | 686 | 0 | 0 |
| division | household | 857 | 37.8% | 0 | 0 | 0 | 857 | 1408 |
| household relationship | person | 2265 | 100.0% | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2265 | 0 |

The tier columns and "not answered" sum to 2265 on every row. "stated, no tier written" is `division` and `relationship`, which the index's vocabulary lists as controlled terms and no card grades.

### A role, or a stated reason for having none

| what the card carries | persons | share |
|---|---:|---:|
| a role, dated and sourced | 327 | 14.4% |
| an occupation, undated | 203 | 9.0% |
| a stated reason for having none | 1735 | 76.6% |
| nothing | 0 | 0.0% |

A stated absence is an answer and it is not a trade. The note on those 1735 cards says why the sources record no occupation — a list of uncalled-for letters prints a name and no trade; the sources of 1835 name the occupations of heads and almost never those of wives, children or the people counted with them.

### The person rows that answer nothing

| person | as the card names them | household | axes left unanswered | what the card gives as the reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| beaubien_household_unnamed | The rest of the Beaubien household, unnamed | hh_beaubien_jean_baptiste | sex, age band | A COLLECTIVE DESCRIPTION NAMES NOBODY, so there is no sex to draw. |
| beaubien_mark_household | Mark Beaubien's family, unnamed | hh_beaubien_mark | sex, age band | A COLLECTIVE DESCRIPTION NAMES NOBODY, so there is no sex to draw. |

Named rather than summed away, which is the whole of this section. Every reason printed here is the one its own card gives, and no row argues with itself: `harmon_daughter_burley` carried a collective refusal over a row naming one woman until T-1395 gave her the refusal her own evidence earns — a household's child word and a later source's married style, neither dated to the scene and neither an age.

### The unplaced, by the roll they were minted off

| roll | persons | share of the unplaced |
|---|---:|---:|
| letter_list | 743 | 52.8% |
| civic | 575 | 40.8% |
| documented | 72 | 5.1% |
| placed | 10 | 0.7% |
| unclaimed | 8 | 0.6% |

1408 persons in households standing `unplaced`. `unplaced` is a term in the index's own division vocabulary and it is a statement — the sources do not place this household — but it is not a division, so this section counts it unanswered.

*Every count here is re-derived from the cards by `--build`; `--check` refuses a hand-edit. The axes are the nine T-1393 names and the tool refuses a rebuild whose answered and unanswered rows do not sum to the layer's persons on each of them.*

## Sex

2259 of 2265 persons (99.7%) carry a sex, AT THREE DIFFERENT TIERS and the table below is the only honest way to read them together: 1082 because a source records it, 590 read off a gendered title, a period contraction or a forename that stands in one sex's naming only, and 587 DRAWN at the male rate measured on the roll the person was named off. A drawn sex is not evidence about that person and never becomes any; the 6 left are collective descriptions that name nobody.

### Sex by the rule that says so

| sex | persons | share | recorded | inferred_title | inferred_contraction | inferred_forename | reconstructed_from_the_roll | unknown |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| male | 1648 | 72.8% | 534 | 0 | 32 | 531 | 551 | 0 |
| female | 611 | 27.0% | 548 | 0 | 0 | 27 | 36 | 0 |
| unknown | 6 | 0.3% | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 6 |

### The rules themselves

| rule | persons | share | what it reads |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| recorded | 1082 | 47.8% | a source records the sex on the card, and no pass had to read it |
| inferred_title | 0 | 0.0% | the read name carries a gendered title (Mrs, Miss, Widow, Mr) |
| inferred_contraction | 32 | 1.4% | the name is a period contraction every attested expansion of which is one sex's |
| inferred_forename | 558 | 24.6% | the forename stands in exactly one sex's naming and in no other (T-1303's derived table, which refuses a name its own evidence splits) |
| reconstructed_from_the_roll | 587 | 25.9% | DRAWN at the male rate measured on the roll this person was named off, seeded by their own id (T-1304) — never evidence about this person |
| unknown | 6 | 0.3% | the name is an initial no roll rate reaches, or a collective description that names nobody |

*A rank or a professional style — Capt., Rev., Dr. — is NOT read as male here. In this town it would almost always be right and it would still be an inference about the period rather than about the person; the age axis takes the honest half of that reading instead.*

## Age

64 of 2265 persons carry a year or an age off a source; 622 more are placed in an adult band by what they are recorded DOING — a poll, a tax list, a muster, a trade, an office, a marriage — and 1579 carry nothing that bears on age at all. SINCE T-1304 EVERY ONE OF THEM CARRIES AN AGE BAND, and the tier table below says which of the three it stands on. The bands are the 1840 Chicago schedule's and a drawn one is never turned into a year. THIS IS STILL NOT AN AGE PYRAMID OF THE TOWN: these are the people the rolls name, overwhelmingly adult men, and the women and children the pyramid lacks are T-1174's brief.

### Age bands

| band | persons | share | what it means |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| birth_year_known | 64 | 2.8% | a source gives the year, however loosely |
| age_stated | 0 | 0.0% | a source gives an age at 1 July 1835 |
| adult_by_civic_list | 192 | 8.5% | an adult: a voter, a taxpayer or a man of militia age |
| adult_by_role | 415 | 18.3% | an adult: a trade, profession or office is not held by a child |
| adult_by_marriage_or_parenthood | 15 | 0.7% | an adult on the register's own face |
| child_by_baptism | 0 | 0.0% | a child |
| unknown | 1579 | 69.7% | nothing on the card bears on age at all |

### The age band every person now carries, by tier

| tier | persons | share | what it means |

## Origin and arrival

Every household carries an arrival block and 95.3% of them (1327) hold a `not_later_than` BOUND rather than an arrival: somebody was writing to that name at Chicago by a date, and nothing says when they came. Only 1393 households name an origin and 1393 a reason for coming.

### The three axes, by tier

| axis | households | share | attested | inferred | reconstructed | unknown |

| arrival date or bound | 1393 | 100.0% | 51 | 1203 | 139 | 0 |
| reason for coming | 1393 | 100.0% | 12 | 13 | 1368 | 0 |

*`canal_and_harbour_works` and `land_purchase` are both 0. The harbour works were the largest employer in the town in 1835 and the land sales were why much of the transient population was here; no household in the known layer states either as its reason. That silence is T-1178's brief, not a finding about the town.*

*A household is classed a house of entertainment when the roof it names carries one of the 7 function terms that mean lodging for pay: `boarding_house`, `hotel`, `large_boarding_house`, `medium_boarding_house`, `small_boarding_house`, `small_inn_or_tavern`, `tavern_inn`. The test used to name `tavern`, `inn` and `coffee_house`, which the function vocabulary cannot spell and no record ever used, so the taverns counted as dwellings (T-1323).*

*No household in the known layer lodges on a vessel. The crews ashore on 1 July 1835 are T-1178's cohort and none of them is named here.*

*NOBODY'S COMMUNITY IS READ OFF THEIR NAME HERE. A surname pool is how T-1177 will RECONSTRUCT the Irish, German, French-Canadian, Métis, Native and free Black town the register implies; it is not evidence about a person the sources merely name, and using it as evidence would manufacture exactly the attributions that ticket is required to mark as reconstructed.*

*What the sources are silent about, in this layer: every child, every servant and apprentice, the garrison below officer rank, the harbour-works gang, the crews in port, and the women of nearly every household the returns name.*

## What the town should have held

Every axis above is a count of the KNOWN layer. This is the list of what it is missing, by bucket and by the ticket that owns it. THERE ARE NO NUMBERS IN THIS SECTION ON PURPOSE: the quantities are T-1293's model and T-1166's order book, and a number typed here would be a second, unsourced answer to the same question.

### The buckets

| bucket | the ticket that owns it |
|---|---|
| the women and children of the households the returns name | T-1170, T-1171, T-1174 |
| servants, apprentices, journeymen and clerks | T-1171, T-1183, T-1189 |
| the labouring trades: labourers, carpenters, teamsters, sawyers, boatmen | T-1347 |
| boarders, lodgers and hotel guests, and the beds they filled | T-1175 |
| the Fort Dearborn garrison below officer rank, and soldiers' families | T-1176 |
| the Native, Métis, free Black, Irish and German town the register implies | T-1177 |
| the transient population: land-sale visitors, the harbour gang, crews ashore | T-1178 |
| the stores, shops, professions and lodging houses with no proprietor named | T-1184 to T-1188 |
